Turkey has sided with both regional and universal comprehensive contracts and protocols in the field of human rights law and humanitarian law. Turkey has sided with contracts on fundamental rights and freedoms, agreements on anti-racial discrimination and minority rights, contracts on gender equality and women's rights, contracts on torture, degrading treatment and disappearances, contracts on child rights contracts, agreements on freedom of organization, contracts on labour and forced labour, contracts on education, contracts on refugees and asylum, contracts on contracts on armed conflict law and dozens of contracts and protocols that may be classified as regional human rights contracts. Public resources should not be allowed to go to waste because of tenders, which have been cancelled for reasons such as property and land supply issues or lack of coordination, as an excuse to do business quickly. Examples show that TOKI cannot exercise that authority in a measured and careful manner. For these reasons, a parliamentary investigation is needed to assess the extent of the damage caused by the TOKI's tenders and determine the measures to be taken. Turkey to the chairman of the Grand National Assembly In order to assess the results of the problems and distribution privatisations caused by the failure of judicial decisions on the loss of illegal collection in the electricity distribution system, we would like to submit a parliamentary investigation into Articles 104 and 105 of the Constitution under Articles 104 and 105 of the Constitution to determine measures to be taken. 1) Cetin Osman Budak [[Antalya]] [[Slap]] [[İstanbul]] 4) Mustafa Sezgin Tanrıkulu [[İstanbul]] [[Balıkesir]] [[Sivas]] [[Manisa]] [[Scholarship]] [[Denizli]] [[İstanbul]] 11) Tur Star Reaper [[Manisa]] [[Ankara]] [[Artvin]] [[40s.]] [[Tekirdağ]] [[Scholarship]] [[Aydın]] [[Ankara]] [[Malatya]] [[İstanbul]] [[40s.]] [[Zonguldak]] [[Burdur]] In Article 138 of the Constitution, the administration and the executive bodies of the Constitution must comply with the court's decisions, which cannot be changed by any means, and the number of judicial decisions that have not been carried out despite its decree is increasing every day. One example of this is the collection of missing fugitives from electrical bills. AKP governments have privatised 21 power distribution companies at $12.7 billion. Many questions have come up during and after the transfer of distribution companies to buyers. As the problems of unfair and illegal collections from citizens, the loss of fugitives and power cuts are becoming increasingly severe, the Ministry of Energy and Resources and the Energy Market Organization (EPDK) are constantly making arrangements in favour of companies, preferring to put new burdens on citizens' electricity bills. Courts have final rulings on the absence of illegal collection of electricity bills. Candidate and selection The president of Madde 101-President is elected directly from among Turkish citizens, who are 40 years old, who have studied high school, who are qualified to be elected MP. The President's mandate is five years. One person can be elected President twice at most. Political party groups can nominate at least 100,000 voters to the presidency, with political parties alone or together with at least five per cent of the total valid vote in the latest general elections. The President-elected MP's membership in Turkey's Grand National Assembly ends. In the election with the general vote, the candidate President will be elected the only majority of the valid votes. If that majority cannot be reached in the first vote, the second vote will be held on the second Sunday following this vote. Two candidates who received the most votes in the first vote, and the candidate President, who received the majority of the valid votes, is elected. If one of the candidates who qualified for the second vote fails to participate in the election for any reason, the second vote is carried out on the basis of the first vote. If the second vote remains a single candidate, the vote will be held in the form of a referendum. The candidate will be elected President if the only majority of the valid votes is received. In the vote, only the election of the President will be renewed if the candidate fails to get the majority of the valid votes. If the elections fail, the current President's position will continue until the new one is in office. Other procedures and foundations for presidential elections are regulated by law.
